{"parentUuid":"d6312a2f-485b-4f1e-bb16-86093d347ba5","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a26af16d000efa903","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- craftsmanship-init: Rigorously read a project codebase and emit a tailored craftsmanship-audit config. Detects languages, frameworks, existing tool configs, and exclusions, then calibrates adaptive thresholds (long_method_loc / large_class_loc / long_parameter_list) from the project's actual p90 metrics — never generic defaults. Smoke-tests required tooling (ruff/eslint/gocyclo/cargo) and fails hard with a precise reason on any gap. Multi-language (Python, TS/JS, Go, Rust). Use this BEFORE running the loop-craftsmanship-audit plugin so the audit is 100% aligned with the project's conventions.\n- file-issue: Abre um issue no tracker (GitHub/Linear/Jira) com o MÁXIMO de contexto acionável para o time resolver sem ida-e-volta. Use SEMPRE que for reportar um bug/achado — inclui dedup-check, repro numerado, evidência (console/rede/logs), causa-raiz provável, severidade, escopo/incerteza honesta e issues relacionados. Nunca inclui secrets. Sintaxe livre — descreva o achado.\n- framer: Use when the user wants to design, edit, or publish a website or web page — creating layouts, editing sections, updating text or images, managing CMS collections and content, syncing external data, creating or modifying code components, managing color and text styles, handling localization, or publishing deployments. Trigger when the user mentions Framer, references their website or web pages, asks to edit designs, update site content, or work with any Framer project — even if they don't explicitly say 'Framer'. **Mandatory precondition**: run `npx @framer/agent@latest setup` and let it complete **BEFORE** loading this skill.\n- framer-code-components: Framer code component implementation guidance, platform constraints, layout annotations, property controls, and authoring best practices. Use only after loading the framer skill and the generated framer-project-<projectId> skill first in the same task; never load this skill directly as the entry point.\n- analysis: PhD-level trajectory validation with empirical evidence — benchmarks, complexity metrics, architecture fitness, scalability projections. Opt-in per project via rules/analysis-config.txt. Runs 6 analysis modules (A1-A6) weighted by project profile (engine/api/library/cli/infrastructure). Produces hypothesis-driven report with quantitative evidence, not opinions. Independent cycle — does not gate the main chain.\n- ast-grep: Structural search and refactor across multi-language codebases via tree-sitter patterns. Use when you need queries Grep can't express — function signatures, class hierarchies, decorator + function, call sites, async patterns, type definitions. Especially useful inside knowledge-base/references/ during /discover-execute. Composable from any context.\n- auto-plan: End-to-end autonomous orchestrator for cycle-discover + cycle-plan + cycle-implement + cycle-code-quality + cycle-review + cycle-release. Single entry-point chains the whole pipeline from idea to a release PR awaiting human approval. Default is full-pipeline; --plan-only retains the legacy discover+plan behavior. Depth (none/light/full) is derived deterministically from a confidence score against repo state — no interactive prompts. MUST-FIX items from /edge-case-plan are auto-injected into the plan before /plan-confidence re-scores. Inspired by planning-with-files v2.43.0 autonomy + composes Claude Code primitives (/plan-goal, /plan-loop) absorbed 2026-05-26.\n- code-quality: Audit project code for dead symbols, fabricated APIs, cross-package orphans, and weak test quality across Go, Python, TypeScript, and Rust (per rules/code-quality-languages.txt enablement). Auto-detects manifests; runs knip + vulture + cargo-udeps + deadcode + tree-sitter symbol fabrication checks + ast-grep cross-package wiring + stryker/mutmut mutation testing. Read-only by design (never edits code). Use after /implement completes its halt-loop, before /review begins — or standalone for periodic audits.\n- deck: Create complete presentation decks with diagrams. Orchestrates /marp-slide and /excalidraw into a single workflow — plans structure, creates diagrams, builds slides, renders HTML + PPTX. Use when the user wants a full presentation with visuals.\n- deps-audit: Audit project dependencies for known vulnerabilities (CVEs) and outdated versions across npm, Python, Rust, Go. Auto-detects manifests; runs osv-scanner + npm audit + npm outdated + pip-audit + cargo audit + govulncheck; cross-references a plan's\n- discover-confidence: Score a blueprint produced by /discover-execute for structural quality (M2 deterministic check). Mirrors /plan-confidence but with a blueprint-shape rubric (research coverage, reference citations, completeness, smells).\n- discover-edge-cases: Analyzes a discovery plan and identifies unforeseen edge cases — focused on deep research over sources declared in the plan (local references under .claude/knowledge-base/references/ and/or allowlisted URLs). Pragmatic — flags real risks without complicating the plan. Use after /discover-plan or when reviewing any plan under .claude/knowledge-base/discoveries/plans/.\n- discover-execute: Executes a discovery plan via halt-loop (ralph-loop-style autonomous iteration) over the sources declared in the plan — local references under knowledge-base/references/ e/ou URLs allowlisted via rules/discover-web-allowlist.txt. Produces a technical blueprint at knowledge-base/discoveries/blueprints/{slug}-blueprint.md. Use after /discover-edge-cases has approved the plan.\n- discover-improve: Iteratively improve a blueprint's discover-confidence score by applying deterministic fixes + LLM-driven semantic fixes via a halt-loop (ralph-loop-style autonomous iteration). Mirrors /plan-improve but for blueprints.\n- discover-plan: Turn the current context + any reference projects under knowledge-base/references/ + allowlisted external sources into a discovery plan saved at knowledge-base/discoveries/plans/{slug}-plan.md. Use when you want to plan a deep-research investigation into a technology or pattern before producing a blueprint.\n- discover-plan-confidence: Score a discovery plan produced by /discover-plan for structural quality (M2 deterministic check). Sibling of /discover-confidence with a discovery-plan-shape rubric (research coverage, reference citations, plan completeness, smells). Use after /discover-edge-cases, before /discover-execute.\n- dogfood: Honesty gate that blocks any 'production-ready' / 'production-grade' / v1.0 claim without recorded evidence of sustained internal use. Reads the project's dogfood manifest at knowledge-base/dogfood/manifest.md plus evidence files at knowledge-base/dogfood/evidence/, applies the project-specific dogfood golden rule (rules/dogfood-golden-rule.md — created per project), and emits EVIDENCE_SUFFICIENT / EVIDENCE_WITH_CAVEATS / EVIDENCE_INSUFFICIENT. Invoke before any production claim, milestone promotion targeting v1.0, README/CHANGELOG edit that touches status language, or release decision.\n- edge-case-plan: Analyzes an implementation plan and identifies unforeseen edge cases. Pragmatic — flags real risks without complicating the design. Use after /to-plan or when reviewing any plan in knowledge-base/plans/.\n- excalidraw: Create Excalidraw diagram JSON files that make visual arguments. Use when the user wants to visualize workflows, architectures, or concepts (standalone or as input to /marp-slide and /deck).\n- frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- grill-me: Interview the user one question at a time until shared understanding on a plan/feature/design is reached. Walks the decision tree branch by branch, resolving dependencies between decisions. For every question, explores the codebase first (Grep/Read) if the answer is there; only asks the user when the answer requires intent/preference/business context. Persists the conversation to knowledge-base/grills/{slug}-grill.md as input for /to-plan. Use BEFORE /to-plan when the feature is non-trivial AND the user has not yet articulated requirements precisely, or when the user explicitly says \"grill me\". SKIP for trivial fixes or when /to-plan can already synthesize from context.\n- implement: Executes an implementation plan from cycle-plan via halt-loop (ralph-loop) with TDD discipline + wiring triad (caller + integration test + runtime metric) + quality gates (SOLID, Clean Code, DRY, Design Patterns). Single entry-point for cycle-implement. Use after /to-plan chain returned verdict ≥ SHIPPABLE_WITH_CAVEATS while working on `develop`.\n- implement-m0-walking-skeleton-sepa-knowledge: Domain knowledge skill paired with the SEPA agent for plan m0-walking-skeleton. Consult ALWAYS during /implement cycle when reasoning about TDD, SOLID, Clean Code, DRY, design patterns, OR wiring triad — this skill hydrates community best practices via WebSearch on top of plan-specific context (ADRs + edge-case findings + project rules). Triggering phrases: \"review this against community standards\", \"what's the canonical pattern\", \"is this idiomatic\", \"best practice for Ink terminal UI components, ink-testing-library snapshot determinism, ESM-only TypeScript library packaging, tsup dts build, terminal render benchmark\".\n- marp-slide: Create Marp presentation slides with embedded CSS theme — outputs self-contained .md + rendered .html + .pptx. Use when the user wants slides only (no full deck with diagrams; for that use /deck).\n- plan-confidence: Score a plan produced by /to-plan for structural quality (M2 deterministic check). Sibling of /discover-confidence with a plan-shape rubric. Use after /edge-case-plan, before /implement.\n- plan-help: Show all available Cycle ecosystem commands with descriptions and recommended flows. Use when the user asks for help, wants to know what commands are available, or says \"what can you do\" / \"help\" / \"list commands\".\n- plan-improve: Iteratively improve a /to-plan plan's M2 score by applying deterministic fixes + LLM-driven semantic fixes via a ralph-loop-style autonomous iteration. Use after /plan-confidence returns a verdict below SHIPPABLE_WITH_CAVEATS (NON_SHIPPABLE or INVALID) and you want the system to attempt auto-improvement before human intervention.\n- quality-init: Rigorously read a project codebase and emit tailored Claude Code quality-gate hooks (PostToolUse) that block code smells on every Write/Edit. Detects languages, frameworks, existing linter configs, and test directories, then calibrates adaptive thresholds (max_complexity / max_function_lines / max_nesting_depth / max_parameters / max_file_lines / duplicate_min_lines) from the project's actual p90 metrics — never generic defaults. Generates Python hook scripts + settings.json patch. Use this BEFORE starting any coding session where you want automatic quality enforcement via hooks.\n- release: Cuts a semver-tagged release from develop → main after /review returns READY_TO_MERGE. Auto-derives version from CHANGELOG sections (major/minor/patch), rewrites [Unreleased] under the new version header, commits chore(release), opens a PR develop→main with rendered release notes, and waits for human approval (the only manual gate — Unbreakable Rule 4). On merge, creates an annotated tag and a GitHub release. Single entry-point for cycle-release. Use after /review {slug} returned READY_TO_MERGE.\n- review: Most rigorous gate of the ecosystem. Validates quality gates + line-by-line plan vs implementation + 100% integration + integration test depth + edge-case coverage. Before starting, generates specialized review agents for the plan's domain (architecture, tests, wiring, cross-validation, domain-specific) and spawns them in parallel. Single entry-point for cycle-review. Use after /implement passed on `develop` and recent commits are ready to be audited (typically before a release cut).\n- roadmap-feature: Add a new feature to an existing ROADMAP.md as the next M<N+1> milestone — without renumbering anything that came before. Reads the existing roadmap, detects the next free milestone ID, runs a 4-question focused grill (why now / dependencies / DoD / risks), cross-checks against the existing \"Explicitly out of scope\" section, optionally clones additional SOTA references when the feature needs peers not already covered, and appends one new `## M<N+1> — [ ]` block to ROADMAP.md plus a `[Unreleased] § Added` entry to CHANGELOG.md. Sister skill of /roadmap-init — same isolation contract, opposite trigger (refuses if ROADMAP.md does NOT exist). Cap is open at M9, M10, M11… extend freely.\n- roadmap-init: Bootstrap a new project from a one-line idea into a macro ROADMAP.md + a curated set of SOTA reference repositories cloned under knowledge-base/references/. Runs a 7-question Socratic interview (problem → users → in-scope → out-of-scope → constraints → success criterion → north-star metric), then researches state-of-the-art peers via web search, gates them by license (warns on GPL/AGPL/BUSL/no-license), clones the approved ones with shallow blob-filter clones, and writes ROADMAP.md (M0-M8 cap, checkbox per milestone) at the repo root. Use ONLY at project inception, BEFORE any /grill-me, /discover-plan, /to-plan or /implement work. REFUSES to run if ROADMAP.md already exists.\n- skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- to-plan: Turn the current conversation context into an implementation plan and save it to knowledge-base/plans/. Use when user wants to create a plan from the current context.\n- deep-research: Deep research harness — fan-out web searches, fetch sources, adversarially verify claims, synthesize a cited report. - When the user wants a deep, multi-source, fact-checked research report on any topic. claude-design:youtube-clipper\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- verify: Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by running the app and observing behavior. Use when asked to verify a PR, confirm a fix works, test a change manually, check that a feature works, or validate local changes before pushing.\n- code-review: Review the current diff for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings). Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo, defaults to 10m) - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; for your working diff use /code-review\n- Rule 9\n125\t> column = why we adopt instead of reinventing. Exact minor pins confirmed by `/deps-audit`\n126\t> against the npm registry + CVE scanners; ranges below are the plan's contract.\n127\t\n128\t| Package | Range | Kind | Purpose | Rule 9 justification (don't reinvent) |\n129\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n130\t| `ink` | `^5.2.0` | dependency | React renderer for terminal | The foundation; roadmap-locked. Building a TUI reconciler is explicitly out of scope (`ROADMAP.md § out of scope`) |\n131\t| `react` | `^18.0.0 \\|\\| ^19.0.0` | peerDependency | component model | Roadmap-locked: react is the ONLY peer (blueprint ADR D1) |\n132\t| `react` (dev) | `^18.3.1` | devDependency | dev/test render target | mirrors ink-ui's dev posture (`references/ink-ui/package.json:50`) |\n133\t| `@types/react` | `^18.3.0` | devDependency | TS types for react 18 | standard DefinitelyTyped |\n134\t| `typescript` | `^5.6.0` | devDependency | strict TS compiler + typecheck gate | industry standard |\n135\t| `tsup` | `^8.3.0` | devDependency | ESM-only build + dts | M0 DoD names tsup (blueprint ADR D3); wraps esbuild + rollup-dts — hand-rolling a dts pipeline violates Rule 9 |\n136\t| `vitest` | `^3.0.0` | devDependency | test runner (roadmap-locked) | standard, fast, ESM-native |\n137\t| `@vitest/coverage-v8` | `^3.0.0` | devDependency | coverage gate | pairs with vitest |\n138\t| `ink-testing-library` | `^4.0.0` | devDependency | render/lastFrame/frames test API | the canonical Ink test lib (react-ink `package.json:65`); **compat with ink 5 confirmed at /deps-audit — if v4 requires ink 6, fall back to `^3.2.0`** |\n139\t| `eslint` | `^9.0.0` | devDependency | lint gate | industry standard (flat config) |\n140\t| `typescript-eslint` | `^8.0.0` | devDependency | TS lint rules | standard TS integration |\n141\t| `prettier` | `^3.3.0` | devDependency | format gate | industry standard |\n142\t| `tsx` | `^4.19.0` | devDependency | bench runner (spawns .bench.tsx) | mirrors react-ink harness (`benchmarks/run.ts:19`) |\n143\t\n144\tTransitives are scanned by `/deps-audit` (osv-scanner + npm audit); no GPL/AGPL transitive allowed\n145\t(`ROADMAP.md § Constraints — Apache-2.0 compatible only`).\n146\t\n147\t## ADRs\n148\t\n149\t> D1–D6 originate in the blueprint (Blueprint §\"ADRs\" carries the full evidence trail); they are\n150\t> restated here in condensed, self-contained form so the plan stands alone. D7–D9 are plan-local.\n151\t\n152\t### D1 — Ink 5 + React 18/19 dual peer, Node ≥ 20 (Ink 6 rejected for M0)\n153\t\n154\t**Decision:** `ink@^5` as direct dependency; `react` peer `^18.0.0 || ^19.0.0`; engines\n155\t`node >= 20`; react is the ONLY peer.\n156\t\n157\t**Rationale:** Ink 6 requires react ≥ 19.2 and node ≥ 22 — both violate the locked roadmap\n158\tconstraints (Node ≥ 20, React 18/19); roadmap also locks \"React is the ONLY required peer\" to\n159\tkeep TTFATT low. Verified by `/deps-audit`: `ink@5.2.1` peers `react >=18.0.0`.\n160\t\n161\t**Alternatives considered:** Ink 6 + React 19 only (rejected: breaks roadmap constraints, narrows\n162\tconsumers to node ≥ 22); ink as peerDependency like react-ink (rejected: one extra install step\n163\tfor every consumer).\n164\t\n165\t**Consequences:** Track ink 5.x until a between-milestones roadmap revision; re-evaluate Ink 6 at M6.\n166\t\n167\t### D2 — Test discipline: vitest + ink-testing-library, FORCE_COLOR pinned, Box-width control, snapshot + semantic double assertion\n168\t\n169\t**Decision:** vitest runner; `ink-testing-library` render; pin `FORCE_COLOR=1` (NO_COLOR/CI\n170\tneutralized) in vitest env; wrap width-sensitive renders in `<Box width={N}>`; one-tick\n171\t`renderFrame` helper; every behavior asserted BOTH via `toMatchSnapshot()` and ≥ 1 plain-text\n172\t`toContain`.\n173\t\n174\t**Rationale:** The analogs prove two opposite-but-deterministic strategies (react-ink plain-text\n175\tvs ink-ui pinned-ANSI exact); flakiness lives only in the unpinned middle. M0 DoD explicitly\n176\trequires snapshot tests.\n177\t\n178\t**Alternatives considered:** ava (rejected: roadmap locks vitest); plain-text-only assertions\n179\t(rejected: DoD requires snapshots); exact-ANSI-only equality (rejected: every M6 theme change\n180\twould rewrite every expected string — DRY violation).\n181\t\n182\t**Consequences:** Snapshots stable across terminals; full NO_COLOR matrix deferred to M6 (M0\n183\tcarries one NO_COLOR smoke test).\n184\t\n185\t### D3 — Build with tsup per M0 DoD lock\n186\t\n187\t**Decision:** tsup (ESM-only, dts on) builds the package.\n188\t\n189\t**Rationale:** The M0 DoD names tsup explicitly and DoD is locked mid-flight\n190\t(`rules/cycle-roadmap.md`: objectives/DoD revised only between milestones); tsup is battle-tested\n191\tfor exactly this shape.\n192\t\n193\t**Alternatives considered:** plain tsc (ecosystem default per the discovery evidence — blocked by\n194\tDoD wording); tsdown (react-ink's engine — younger tool, no DoD backing).\n195\t\n196\t**Consequences:** One extra devDependency; revisit between milestones if friction appears.\n197\t\n198\t### D4 — ChatMessage v0: single component, explicit role prop (no runtime context)\n199\t\n200\t**Decision:** One `ChatMessage` with `role: 'user' | 'assistant'` + `children`; glyph prefix +\n201\ttoken colors from the provider.\n202\t\n203\t**Rationale:** react-ink's context-driven compound decomposition presupposes a runtime store that\n204\tonly arrives at M7 (stream adapter); an explicit prop is discoverable and testable with zero\n205\tonboarding (KISS/YAGNI). gemini-cli ships per-role glyph prefixes in production.\n206\t\n207\t**Alternatives considered:** compound components now (rejected: no second consumer case — Rule of\n208\t3); separate `UserMessage`/`AssistantMessage` (rejected: roadmap names ONE `ChatMessage`\n209\tprimitive; single role-switched component mirrors `@theokit/ui`).\n210\t\n211\t**Consequences:** M1 may split internals without breaking the public `role` prop; parts/tool-calls\n212\tarrive M2+ additively.\n213\t\n214\t### D5 — Theme stub: flat semantic tokens now, component-style layer deferred to M6\n215\t\n216\t**Decision:** `<TheoTUIProvider>` carries a flat semantic-token object (role colors + status)\n217\tconsumed via `useTheoTheme()`; no per-component style functions at M0.\n218\t\n219\t**Rationale:** gemini-cli's semantic-token taxonomy matches the M6 terminal-adaptive goal;\n220\tink-ui's per-component `styles`/`config` + deepmerge is the right extension seam but over-structure\n221\tfor one component (YAGNI — `/code-quality` would rightly flag the dead structure).\n222\t\n223\t**Alternatives considered:** ink-ui full component-theme shape now (rejected: ~90% dead structure\n224\tat M0); no provider at all (rejected: M0 DoD requires the stub; the composition-root seam per\n225\t`rules/architecture.md` must exist from the first commit).\n226\t\n227\t**Consequences:** M0 token names become API — set kept minimal so M6 reorganizes additively.\n228\t\n229\t### D6 — Benchmark: tsx harness, mean/peak ms-per-frame, ≥ 3 measured runs, JSON + docs persistence\n230\t\n231\t**Decision:** react-ink-style custom bench (tsx runner + ink-testing stdout frames +\n232\t`performance.now()`), workload = 100-message thread + 300-token streaming append; protocol =\n233\t1 discarded warmup + ≥ 3 measured runs, mean ± std dev; output = console table AND committed JSON\n234\tunder `docs/benchmarks/`.\n235\t\n236\t**Rationale:** The only metrics-driven prior art is react-ink's harness (ink's own benchmarks\n237\temit no numbers). Statistical rigor per `rules/analysis-golden-rule.md` § 3; persisted artifact\n238\tper `rules/public-copy.md` § 4.\n239\t\n240\t**Alternatives considered:** `vitest bench`/tinybench (rejected: measures ops/sec micro-loops,\n241\tnot frame flushes on a live Ink render — wrong phenomenon); no benchmark at M0 (rejected: cycle\n242\towner requires benchmark data as acceptance signal).\n243\t\n244\t**Consequences:** M0 ships the baseline; later milestones extend the workload against the same\n245\tmetric definitions.\n246\t\n247\t### D7 — Lint/format: eslint 9 (flat) + typescript-eslint + prettier; gates wired as separate scripts\n248\t\n249\t**Decision:** `eslint` flat config with `typescript-eslint` recommended rules for lint;\n250\t`prettier` for format (checked via `--check` in the gate); five separate npm scripts\n251\t(`format:check`, `lint`, `typecheck`, `test`, `build`) plus an aggregate `gates` script running\n252\tthem in DoD order.\n253\t\n254\t**Rationale:** The gate chain in `ROADMAP.md § M0 DoD` names five DISTINCT stages — separate\n255\tscripts keep each stage's exit code observable in CI (fail-fast, fail-clear —\n256\t`rules/error-handling.md § 1`). eslint+prettier is the industry standard pair (Rule 9: don't\n257\tadopt exotic tooling for a community library consumers will contribute to).\n258\t\n259\t**Alternatives considered:** `xo` (ink/ink-ui's choice — rejected: opinionated wrapper hides the\n260\tlint stage's configurability and pins its own prettier fork assumptions); `oxlint` (react-ink\n261\tmonorepo root — rejected: younger tool, no typescript-type-aware rules yet); folding lint into\n262\t`test` like ink-ui does (`\"test\": \"tsc --noEmit && xo && ava\"` — rejected: collapses gate exit\n263\tcodes, CI can't report which stage failed).\n264\t\n265\t**Consequences:** Five deterministic gates; contributors get standard tooling; slight config\n266\toverhead (two config files) accepted.\n267\t\n268\t### D8 — Package manifest is protected by an executable contract test\n269\t\n270\t**Decision:** `tests/package-manifest.test.ts` asserts the packaging invariants (`type: module`,\n271\ttypes-first `exports` with single `.` entry, `files: [\"dist\"]`, `sideEffects: false`,\n272\t`engines.node >= 20`, react as ONLY peer, license Apache-2.0).\n273\t\n274\t**Rationale:** The manifest IS the M0 deliverable — regressions there (a stray CJS field, a\n275\twidened peer set) break consumers silently. An executable contract makes the scaffold TDD-able\n276\t(RED before the manifest exists) and guards M1+ edits. Mirrors the \"tests protect behavior\"\n277\tphilosophy (`rules/testing.md § 1`) applied to packaging behavior.\n278\t\n279\t**Alternatives considered:** publint as a devDependency gate (rejected for M0: scheduled by\n280\troadmap for M8 GA — YAGNI now, and it doesn't check OUR invariants like react-only-peer); no test\n281\t(rejected: scaffold would be the only untested behavior in the milestone).\n282\t\n283\t**Consequences:** package.json edits fail loudly; the test doubles as executable documentation of\n284\tthe packaging contract.\n285\t\n286\t### D9 — Benchmark verdict is data-only at M0 (no pass/fail threshold)\n287\t\n288\t**Decision:** The M0 benchmark records baseline numbers (frames, mean/peak/std-dev ms per frame)\n289\tinto a committed JSON; it does NOT enforce a threshold gate.\n290\t\n291\t**Rationale:** A threshold needs a baseline to exist first — M0 IS the baseline\n292\t(`rules/analysis-golden-rule.md § 7`: first run creates the baseline). Inventing a pass number\n293\tbefore data exists would be fabricated rigor (Rule 3). Statistical protocol (≥ 3 runs, mean ±\n294\tstd dev, hardware note) per `rules/analysis-golden-rule.md § 3`.\n295\t\n296\t**Alternatives considered:** hard ms-per-frame gate in CI (rejected: no baseline yet + CI-runner\n297\tvariance would flake the gate); no benchmark at M0 (rejected: cycle owner requires benchmark\n298\tdata as acceptance signal).\n299\t\n300\t**Consequences:** M1+ can regress against the committed JSON; CI runs the bench in smoke mode\n301\t(1 run) only to prove the harness executes, keeping wall-clock low.\n302\t\n303\t## Drawbacks & Risks\n304\t\n305\t| Drawback / Risk | Severity | Mitigation | Owner |\n306\t|---|---|---|---|\n307\t| `ink-testing-library@^4` may require ink ≥ 6, conflicting with ink ^5 (blueprint flagged: clone carries only ink 6 manifests) | High | `/deps-audit` confirms the peer range against the registry BEFORE implement; fallback pin `^3.2.0` declared in § Dependencies | deps-audit |\n308\t| Ink 5 + React 18 pairing under Node 22 dev machine vs engines `>=20` claim — untested combination until T0.2 | Medium | T0.2's smoke test + gate chain runs on Node 20 in CI matrix (single extra job) to prove the floor | implement |\n309\t| Snapshot tests can still flake if a future test forgets the width/env discipline | Medium | `renderFrame` helper + vitest env pin centralize the discipline; `src/*.test.tsx` must import the helper (review checklist item) | review |\n310\t| tsup adds a build abstraction the ecosystem itself doesn't use (ink/ink-ui use tsc) | Low | ADR D3 documents the DoD lock + the between-milestones revisit trigger | roadmap owner |\n311\t| Benchmark numbers from a dev laptop are not comparable across machines | Low | JSON records hardware + node version + methodology; comparisons only ever against same-machine baselines (`rules/analysis-golden-rule.md § 3`) | implement |\n312\t\n313\t## Unresolved Questions\n314\t\n315\t- Q-U1 — ~~Exact `ink-testing-library` major compatible with `ink@^5`~~ **RESOLVED by\n316\t  `/deps-audit` 2026-07-05:** v4.0.0 is developed against `ink ^5.0.0` (its devDeps) — keep\n317\t  `^4.0.0`; the `^3.2.0` fallback is unnecessary\n318\t  (audit: `knowledge-base/audits/m0-walking-skeleton-deps-audit-2026-07-05.md`).\n319\t- Q-U2 — ~~Exact `ink@5.x` peer-react range~~ **RESOLVED by `/deps-audit` 2026-07-05:**\n320\t  `ink@5.2.1` peers `react >=18.0.0` — the plan's `^18.0.0 || ^19.0.0` is inside the accepted\n321\t  range; keep as declared. ink 5 engines `node >=18`; our `>=20` floor is stricter — compatible.\n322\t\n323\t(none — every decision is resolved at plan time: blueprint ADRs D1–D6, plan ADRs D7–D9, and the\n324\tdeps-audit resolutions above.)\n325\t\n326\t## Critical paths\n327\t\n328\tFor `/code-quality` D4 (mutation) when enabled: `src/chat-message.tsx` (role→glyph/color\n329\tbranching) and `src/theme.tsx` (token merge/fallback logic) are the M0 critical paths.\n330\t\n331\t## Dependency Graph\n332\t\n333\t```\n334\tPhase 0 (toolchain scaffold) ──▶ Phase 1 (theme stub) ──▶ Phase 2 (ChatMessage) ──▶ Phase 3 (bench + example)\n335\t                                                                                          │\n336\t                                                                                          ▼\n337\t                                                                            Phase 4 (CI + language gate)\n338\t                                                                                          │\n339\t                                                                                          ▼\n340\t                                                                            Final Phase (integration validation)\n341\t```\n342\t\n343\tAll phases are sequential (each consumes the previous phase's outputs). No parallel tracks — the\n344\twalking skeleton is one vertical slice.\n345\t\n346\t---\n347\t\n348\t## Phase 0: Toolchain scaffold\n349\t\n350\t**Objective:** An installable, buildable, testable, lintable empty package with licensing and\n351\tchangelog in place.\n352\t\n353\t### T0.1 — Package manifest, licensing, changelog (contract-test first)\n354\t\n355\t#### Objective\n356\tCreate `package.json` (publishable shape), `LICENSE`/`NOTICE` (Apache-2.0), `CHANGELOG.md`\n357\t(`[Unreleased]` seeded), protected by an executable manifest-contract test.\n358\t\n359\t#### Why this step (action + reasoning)\n360\t\n361\t1. **What:** Write the manifest contract test (RED), then `package.json` with the blueprint's\n362\t   locked shape (ESM-only, types-first exports, react-only peer, ink dependency, engines\n363\t   `>=20`), install devDependencies with pnpm, add Apache-2.0 LICENSE + NOTICE and CHANGELOG.md.\n364\t2. **Why now:** Everything else (build, test, lint) hangs off the manifest; it must exist first\n365\t   and be right first — ADR D8 makes it executable-contract-protected so later phases can't\n366\t   silently corrupt it. Licensing lands in the FIRST commit touching distributable code\n367\t   (Apache-2.0 is a roadmap compliance constraint); CHANGELOG discipline is Unbreakable Rule 6.\n368\t\n369\t#### Evidence\n370\t- Exports/files shape: `knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/package.json:16-31`.\n371\t- Peer/engines posture: Blueprint §\"D1\" (ink ^5 dep, react-only peer, node >=20);\n372\t  `knowledge-base/references/ink-ui/package.json:21,59`.\n373\t- Version ranges: § Dependencies table (this plan).\n374\t\n375\t#### Files to edit\n376\t```\n377\ttests/package-manifest.test.ts — (NEW) RED contract test for packaging invariants\n378\tpackage.json                   — (NEW) manifest per ADR D1/D3/D7/D8\n379\tLICENSE                        — (NEW) Apache-2.0 full text\n380\tNOTICE                         — (NEW) \"Copyright 2026 Theo ecosystem contributors\" attribution\n381\tCHANGELOG.md                   — (NEW) Keep-a-Changelog skeleton + [Unreleased] § Added entries\n382\t```\n383\t\n384\t#### Deep file dependency analysis\n385\t- `tests/package-manifest.test.ts`: reads `package.json` from disk (JSON import via `readFileSync`\n386\t  — no build needed); no other file depends on it.\n387\t- `package.json`: consumed by pnpm/tsup/vitest/eslint/CI; its `scripts` are the gate-chain\n388\t  contract for T4.1; its `exports` is the integration-test import target in T2.2.\n389\t- `LICENSE`/`NOTICE`/`CHANGELOG.md`: standalone; `CHANGELOG.md` is later verified by\n390\t  `/implement`'s acceptance gate + `/review` hard gate.\n391\t\n392\t#### Deep Dives\n393\t- Manifest invariants (asserted by the test): `\"type\": \"module\"`; `exports` =\n394\t  `{\".\": {\"types\": \"./dist/index.d.ts\", \"default\": \"./dist/index.js\"}}` exactly (single entry,\n395\t  `types` key listed before `default` — required by Node/TS resolution order);\n396\t  `\"files\": [\"dist\"]`; `\"sideEffects\": false`; `engines.node: \">=20\"`;\n397\t  `peerDependencies` has EXACTLY the keys `react` (+ `@types/react` optional-peer is NOT added at\n398\t  M0 — YAGNI); `license: \"Apache-2.0\"`; `name: \"@theokit/tui\"`; `private` ABSENT.\n399\t- Scripts (contract for T4.1): `build` (tsup), `test` (vitest run), `test:watch`,\n400\t  `typecheck` (tsc --noEmit), `lint` (eslint .), `format` (prettier --write .),\n401\t  `format:check` (prettier --check .), `bench` (tsx benchmarks/run.ts),\n402\t  `gates` (format:check && lint && typecheck && test && build — DoD order).\n403\t- Edge cases: pnpm 10 requires `packageManager` field for corepack-strict CI — set\n404\t  `\"packageManager\": \"pnpm@10.34.1\"` (dev machine version, evidence: local `pnpm --version`).\n405\t\n406\t#### Tasks\n407\t1. Write `tests/package-manifest.test.ts` with all invariant assertions — run: FAILS (no package.json)\n408\t2. Write `package.json` (fields + § Dependencies ranges + scripts)\n409\t3. `pnpm install` — lockfile generated and committed\n410\t4. Add `LICENSE` (Apache-2.0 text), `NOTICE`, `CHANGELOG.md` (`[Unreleased] § Added` entries for scaffold + ChatMessage + provider + benchmark)\n411\t5. Re-run test — GREEN (vitest picks `tests/**` by default config set in T0.2; until then run via `pnpm vitest run tests/package-manifest.test.ts`)\n412\t\n413\t#### TDD\n414\t```\n415\tRED:     package_manifest_declares_esm_only_types_first_exports() — expect(pkg.type).toBe(\"module\"); expect(Object.keys(pkg.exports[\".\"])) first key \"types\" then \"default\"; expect(pkg.files).toEqual([\"dist\"]) (MUST fail: package.json absent)\n416\tRED:     package_manifest_declares_react_as_only_peer_and_ink_as_dependency() — expect(Object.keys(pkg.peerDependencies)).toEqual([\"react\"]); expect(pkg.dependencies.ink).toMatch(/\\^5/)\n417\tRED:     package_manifest_declares_apache2_license_and_node20_floor() — expect(pkg.license).toBe(\"Apache-2.0\"); expect(pkg.engines.node).toBe(\">=20\"); expect(pkg.sideEffects).toBe(false)\n418\tGREEN:   Write package.json satisfying every assertion\n419\tREFACTOR: None expected\n420\tVERIFY:  pnpm vitest run tests/package-manifest.test.ts\n421\t```\n422\t\n423\t#### Concurrency tests\n424\t(none — single-threaded)\n425\t\n426\t#### Acceptance Criteria\n427\t- [ ] `pnpm vitest run tests/package-manifest.test.ts` green\n428\t- [ ] `pnpm install` exits 0; `pnpm-lock.yaml` committed\n429\t- [ ] `LICENSE` contains the full Apache-2.0 text; `NOTICE` names the project\n430\t- [ ] `CHANGELOG.md` has `[Unreleased]` with ≥ 1 entry referencing this plan slug\n431\t- [ ] Pass: size — every new file ≤ 500 lines (`rules/architecture.md` budget; LICENSE exempt as vendored legal text)\n432\t\n433\t#### DoD (Definition of Done)\n434\t- [ ] All tasks completed; manifest contract test green\n435\t- [ ] No type/lint gates yet (arrive T0.2) — explicitly out of this task's scope\n436\t\n437\t### T0.2 — Build + test + lint toolchain (tsconfig, tsup, vitest, eslint, prettier)\n438\t\n439\t#### Objective\n440\t`pnpm build` produces `dist/index.js` + `dist/index.d.ts` from a minimal `src/index.ts`;\n441\t`pnpm test`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm format:check` all run and exit 0.\n442\t\n443\t#### Why this step (action + reasoning)\n444\t\n445\t1. **What:** Write a RED export-surface test (imports `src/index.ts`, asserts the module exposes\n446\t   `VERSION`); add `tsconfig.json` (strict), `tsup.config.ts` (ESM + dts), `vitest.config.ts`\n447\t   (node env + `FORCE_COLOR=1` pin + include `src/**` and `tests/**`), `eslint.config.js`,\n448\t   `.prettierrc.json`, `.prettierignore`; create `src/index.ts` exporting `VERSION`.\n449\t2. **Why now:** The five gates must exist BEFORE any component code so Phase 1/2 land under full\n450\t   discipline (TDD needs a runner; the parsimony ladder's guardrails need lint/typecheck live).\n451\t   `FORCE_COLOR` pin implements blueprint ADR D2's determinism contract at the config root —\n452\t   individual tests can't forget it.\n453\t\n454\t#### Evidence\n455\t- vitest minimal node config: `knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/vitest.config.ts:1-9`.\n456\t- Color pin necessity: `knowledge-base/references/ink-ui/package.json:71` (ink-ui pins\n457\t  FORCE_COLOR for its runner); Blueprint §\"D2\".\n458\t- tsconfig strictness direction: `knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/x-buildutils/ts/base.json:3`\n459\t  (strictest-based), `jsx: react-jsx`, `module: ESNext`, `moduleResolution: bundler`.\n460\t\n461\t#### Files to edit\n462\t```\n463\ttests/export-surface.test.ts — (NEW) RED: import { VERSION } from '../src/index.js' asserts defined\n464\ttsconfig.json                — (NEW) strict TS, react-jsx, ESNext/bundler, noEmit (tsup emits)\n465\ttsup.config.ts               — (NEW) entry src/index.ts, format esm, dts true, clean, target node20\n466\tvitest.config.ts             — (NEW) node env, globals, env pins FORCE_COLOR=\"1\" + NO_COLOR=\"\" + CI=\"\" (EC-5: identical color-detection inputs local vs CI), coverage v8\n467\teslint.config.js             — (NEW) typescript-eslint recommended flat config\n468\t.prettierrc.json             — (NEW) default prettier options (explicit file so editors agree)\n469\t.prettierignore              — (NEW) dist/, pnpm-lock.yaml, docs/benchmarks/*.json, .claude/\n470\tsrc/index.ts                 — (NEW) export const VERSION = \"0.0.0\" (placeholder until T1/T2 add exports)\n471\t```\n472\t\n473\t#### Deep file dependency analysis\n474\t- `tsconfig.json`: consumed by tsc (typecheck gate), eslint type-aware rules, vitest, tsup dts.\n475\t- `vitest.config.ts`: env pin affects ALL tests — the determinism root (ADR D2).\n476\t- `src/index.ts`: the composition root; T1.1/T2.1 append exports; `tests/export-surface.test.ts`\n477\t  grows with them.\n478\t- `eslint.config.js` + `.prettierrc.json`: consumed by gates; `.prettierignore` protects the\n479\t  committed benchmark JSON from format churn.\n480\t\n481\t#### Deep Dives\n482\t- tsconfig compilerOptions: `strict: true`, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess: true`,\n483\t  `exactOptionalPropertyTypes: true` (strictest-inspired), `jsx: \"react-jsx\"`,\n484\t  `module: \"ESNext\"`, `moduleResolution: \"bundler\"`, `target: \"ES2022\"`,\n485\t  `lib: [\"ES2022\"]`, `types: [\"node\"]` only where needed, `skipLibCheck: true`,\n486\t  `isolatedModules: true`, `noEmit: true` (tsup emits; typecheck stays pure).\n487\t- Import hygiene: source imports use `.js` extension style compatible with `bundler` resolution;\n488\t  tests import from `../src/index.js`.\n489\t- Edge cases: vitest `environment: \"node\"`; `passWithNoTests: false` (a silent empty suite is a\n490\t  broken gate — fail-fast); coverage provider v8 with `include: [\"src/**\"]`.\n491\t\n492\t#### Tasks\n493\t1. Write `tests/export-surface.test.ts` — FAILS (src/index.ts absent)\n494\t2. `pnpm typecheck` 0 errors; `pnpm lint` 0 warnings; `pnpm format:check` clean\n513\t- [ ] Pass: complexity — every file cyclomatic complexity <= 10, verified by `pnpm lint` exit 0 (complexity rule enabled)\n514\t- [ ] Pass: size — `wc -l` on every changed source file reports <= 500 lines\n515\t\n516\t#### DoD (Definition of Done)\n517\t- [ ] All five gate scripts individually exit 0\n518\t- [ ] `pnpm gates` aggregate exits 0\n519\t- [ ] CHANGELOG updated\n520\t\n521\t---\n522\t\n523\t## Phase 1: Theme stub\n524\t\n525\t**Objective:** Flat semantic tokens + `<TheoTUIProvider>` + `useTheoTheme()` with default-theme\n526\tfallback, exported from the public entry.\n527\t\n528\t### T1.1 — Semantic tokens, TheoTUIProvider, useTheoTheme\n529\t\n530\t#### Objective\n531\tImplement `src/theme.tsx`: `TheoTheme` type (flat semantic tokens), `defaultTheme`,\n532\t`ThemeContext`, `<TheoTUIProvider theme?>`, `useTheoTheme()`; export all from `src/index.ts`.\n533\t\n534\t#### Why this step (action + reasoning)\n535\t\n536\t1. **What:** RED tests for hook fallback (no provider → defaultTheme), provider passthrough\n537\t   (custom theme reaches consumers), and partial-override merge; then implement the minimal\n538\t   context module and wire exports.\n539\t2. **Why it is necessary now:** ChatMessage (Phase 2) consumes tokens via the hook (DIP —\n540\t   Baseline § Architecture boundaries); building the component first would hard-code colors and\n541\t   force a rework. Blueprint ADR D5 locks the flat-token shape; gemini-cli's taxonomy\n542\t   (`semantic-tokens.ts:9-43`) is the naming source.\n543\t\n544\t#### Evidence\n545\t- Context+hook consumption pattern: `knowledge-base/references/ink-ui/source/theme.tsx:63-68`\n546\t  (`useComponentTheme` reads `useContext(ThemeContext)`).\n547\t- Token taxonomy: `knowledge-base/references/gemini-cli/packages/cli/src/ui/themes/semantic-tokens.ts:9-43`\n548\t  (text/status categories).\n549\t- Deliberate M0 simplification (flat, no per-component styles): Blueprint §\"D5\".\n550\t\n551\t#### Files to edit\n552\t```\n553\tsrc/theme.test.tsx — (NEW) RED tests (fallback, passthrough, partial override)\n554\tsrc/theme.tsx      — (NEW) TheoTheme type + defaultTheme + provider + hook\n555\tsrc/index.ts       — add exports { TheoTUIProvider, useTheoTheme, defaultTheme, type TheoTheme }\n556\ttests/export-surface.test.ts — extend: new exports asserted\n557\t```\n558\t\n559\t#### Deep file dependency analysis\n560\t- `src/theme.tsx`: no internal deps (domain layer); depends on `react` only (createContext,\n561\t  useContext) — deliberately NOT on ink (tokens are renderer-agnostic).\n562\t- `src/index.ts`: gains 4 exports; `tests/export-surface.test.ts` and (later)\n563\t  `tests/public-api.integration.test.tsx` are the callers.\n564\t- `src/theme.test.tsx`: co-located per `rules/testing.md § 5` (`<name>.test.<ext>` JS/TS convention).\n565\t\n566\t#### Deep Dives\n567\t- Data structure (exact fields, per blueprint ADR D5 — role colors + status, nothing more):\n568\t\n569\t```pseudocode\n570\ttype TheoTheme = {\n571\t  role: {\n572\t    user:      { prefix: string; glyph: string; text: string };\n573\t    assistant: { prefix: string; glyph: string; text: string };\n574\t  };\n575\t  status: { error: string; success: string; warning: string };\n576\t};\n577\tdefaultTheme: user glyph \"> \" cyan, assistant glyph \"✦ \" magenta, text undefined-color-safe\n578\tTheoTUIProvider({ theme?: PartialDeep<TheoTheme>, children }) -> merges over defaultTheme (shallow per top-level group — no deepmerge dep at M0)\n579\tuseTheoTheme(): TheoTheme -> useContext(ThemeContext) ?? defaultTheme\n580\t```\n581\t\n582\t- Invariants: `useTheoTheme()` NEVER returns undefined (fallback to defaultTheme — fail-safe\n583\t  default, `rules/error-handling.md § 2` explicit over magic null); merge is shallow at the\n584\t  top-level groups (`role`, `status`) — documented limitation, deepmerge arrives with M6 (YAGNI).\n585\t- Edge cases: provider with `theme={undefined}` === no override; partial override of one role\n586\t  keeps the other role's defaults (test asserts).\n587\t\n588\t#### Tasks\n589\t1. Write the 4 RED tests in `src/theme.test.tsx`\n590\t2. Implement `src/theme.tsx` minimal (parsimony rung 6)\n591\t3. Export from `src/index.ts`; extend export-surface test\n592\t4. CHANGELOG entry\n593\t\n594\t#### TDD\n595\t```\n596\tRED:     use_theo_theme_returns_default_tokens_without_provider() — probe Ink component renders the hook value; expect(frame).toContain(defaultTheme.role.user.glyph)\n597\tRED:     provider_passes_custom_theme_to_consumers() — custom assistant glyph; expect(frame).toContain(\"◆ \")\n598\tRED:     partial_override_preserves_untouched_token_groups() — override role.user only; expect(theme.status.error).toBe(defaultTheme.status.error); expect(theme.role.assistant).toEqual(defaultTheme.role.assistant)\n599\tRED:     empty_theme_override_yields_default_tokens() — <TheoTUIProvider theme={{}}>; expect(theme).toEqual(defaultTheme) (EC-4: empty-but-valid extreme of the merge)\n600\tGREEN:   Implement theme.tsx until all four pass\n601\tREFACTOR: Extract mergeTheme helper only if the inline merge exceeds ~10 lines (KISS)\n602\tVERIFY:  pnpm vitest run src/theme.test.tsx\n603\t```\n604\t\n605\t#### Concurrency tests\n606\t(none — single-threaded)\n607\t\n608\t#### Acceptance Criteria\n609\t- [ ] `pnpm vitest run src/theme.test.tsx tests/export-surface.test.ts` exits 0 with 4 theme tests + extended export assertions passing\n610\t- [ ] `useTheoTheme` returns a total `TheoTheme` in all cases (no undefined leaks — typecheck proves)\n611\t- [ ] Pass: quality — `pnpm lint` exits 0 with 0 warnings; `wc -l src/theme.tsx` <= 500\n612\t\n613\t#### DoD (Definition of Done)\n614\t- [ ] `pnpm gates` exits 0\n615\t- [ ] CHANGELOG updated\n616\t\n617\t---\n618\t\n619\t## Phase 2: ChatMessage primitive\n620\t\n621\t**Objective:** `ChatMessage` renders user/assistant messages with role glyph + token colors,\n622\tsnapshot-covered including NO_COLOR, integrated through the public entry.\n623\t\n624\t### T2.1 — ChatMessage component (snapshot + semantic tests first)\n625\t\n626\t#### Objective\n627\tImplement `src/chat-message.tsx`: `ChatMessage({ role, children })` rendering\n628\t`<Box>` + glyph prefix `<Text>` + content `<Text>`, colored via `useTheoTheme()`.\n629\t\n630\t#### Why this step (action + reasoning)\n631\t\n632\t1. **What:** RED tests — snapshot of `lastFrame()` per role, semantic `toContain` assertions,\n633\t   NO_COLOR smoke test, and a shared `renderFrame` helper (one React tick); then the minimal\n634\t   component.\n635\t2. **Why now:** This is the milestone's user-visible primitive; it lands AFTER the theme (its\n636\t   only dependency, per DIP) and BEFORE the benchmark (which renders it). API shape locked by\n637\t   blueprint ADR D4 (explicit `role` prop, no runtime context).\n638\t\n639\t#### Evidence\n640\t- Glyph idiom in production: `knowledge-base/references/gemini-cli/packages/cli/src/ui/components/messages/UserMessage.tsx:26`\n641\t  (2-char `\"> \"` prefix), `GeminiMessage.tsx:28` (`\"✦ \"`).\n642\t- One-tick helper: `knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/src/tests/helpers.tsx:13-17`.\n643\t- Width control: `knowledge-base/references/ink-ui/test/progress-bar.tsx:10-12`.\n644\t- Double-assertion strategy (snapshot + toContain): Blueprint §\"D2\".\n645\t\n646\t#### Files to edit\n647\t```\n648\tsrc/chat-message.test.tsx — (NEW) RED: snapshots + semantic + NO_COLOR\n649\ttests/helpers.tsx          — (NEW) renderFrame(node): render + one tick + lastFrame\n650\tsrc/chat-message.tsx       — (NEW) the component\n651\tsrc/index.ts               — add export { ChatMessage, type ChatMessageProps }\n652\ttests/export-surface.test.ts — extend\n653\t```\n654\t\n655\t#### Deep file dependency analysis\n656\t- `src/chat-message.tsx`: imports `ink` (Box, Text) + `./theme.js` (hook). First production\n657\t  consumer of the theme — proves the DIP seam.\n658\t- `tests/helpers.tsx`: imported by `src/chat-message.test.tsx` and\n659\t  `tests/public-api.integration.test.tsx` (T2.2) — single determinism point.\n660\t- `src/index.ts`: gains `ChatMessage`; benchmark + example are downstream callers (Phase 3).\n661\t\n662\t#### Deep Dives\n663\t- Signature:\n664\t\n665\t```pseudocode\n666\ttype ChatMessageProps = { role: \"user\" | \"assistant\"; children: ReactNode };\n667\tfunction ChatMessage({ role, children }): \n668\t  tokens = useTheoTheme().role[role]\n669\t  return <Box> <Text color={tokens.prefix}>{tokens.glyph}</Text> <Text color={tokens.text}>{children}</Text> </Box>\n670\t\n671\t# Example\n672\tinput:  <ChatMessage role=\"user\">hello</ChatMessage>  (defaultTheme, FORCE_COLOR=1)\n673\toutput: \"> hello\" with \"> \" cyan-colored (ANSI in snapshot), text default color\n674\t```\n675\t\n676\t- Invariants: role is a closed union — TS exhaustiveness (`noUncheckedIndexedAccess` keeps\n677\t  `theme.role[role]` total); component renders in ONE line for short content (wrapping is Ink's\n678\t  concern, not asserted at M0 beyond the fixed-width snapshot).\n679\t- Edge cases: empty children (renders glyph only — snapshot); NO_COLOR run must contain the same\n680\t  visible text (glyph + content) with zero ANSI escapes (assert `\u001b[` absent).\n681\t\n682\t#### Tasks\n683\t1. Write `tests/helpers.tsx` (renderFrame)\n684\t2. Write RED tests in `src/chat-message.test.tsx` (7 tests below)\n685\t3. Implement `src/chat-message.tsx` minimal\n686\t4. Export from index; extend export-surface test\n687\t5. CHANGELOG entry\n688\t\n689\t#### TDD\n690\t```\n691\tRED:     renders_user_message_with_glyph_prefix() — expect(frame).toContain(\"> \"); expect(frame).toContain(\"hello\")\n692\tRED:     renders_assistant_message_with_glyph_prefix() — expect(frame).toContain(\"✦ \"); expect(frame).toContain(content)\n693\tRED:     user_and_assistant_frames_match_snapshot_under_forced_color() — expect(frame).toMatchSnapshot() per role inside <Box width={40}>\n694\tRED:     respects_custom_theme_glyph_via_provider() — provider override; expect(frame).toContain(\"◆ \")\n695\tRED:     no_color_render_contains_text_without_ansi_escapes() — spawn-free: re-render with vitest.stubEnv NO_COLOR=1 + FORCE_COLOR unset; assert no \"\u001b[\" and content present\n696\tRED:     invalid_role_throws_typed_error_with_clear_message() — (EC-1, MUST FIX) render with role=\"system\" as any; expect(renderIt).toThrow(TypeError); error message should contain \"ChatMessage\" naming the invalid value and the accepted union\n697\tRED:     long_content_wraps_inside_narrow_box_without_crash() — (EC-3) 120-char string in <Box width={20}>; expect(frame.length > 0).toBe(true); expect(frame).toContain(firstWord)\n698\tGREEN:   Implement chat-message.tsx (including the 3-line role guard at component entry — rules/error-handling.md § 2 boundary validation) until all pass\n699\tREFACTOR: None expected beyond glyph/color constant extraction into theme defaults (already there)\n700\tVERIFY:  pnpm vitest run src/chat-message.test.tsx\n701\t```\n702\t\n703\t#### Concurrency tests\n704\t(none — single-threaded)\n705\t\n706\t#### Acceptance Criteria\n707\t- [ ] 7 tests green; snapshots committed and stable across two consecutive runs (`pnpm test` twice)\n708\t- [ ] NO_COLOR smoke — `pnpm vitest run src/chat-message.test.tsx -t no_color` exits 0 proving the frame contains the text with 0 ANSI escapes\n709\t- [ ] Pass: quality — `pnpm lint` exits 0 with 0 warnings; `wc -l src/chat-message.tsx` <= 500\n710\t\n711\t#### DoD (Definition of Done)\n712\t- [ ] `pnpm gates` exits 0\n713\t- [ ] CHANGELOG updated\n714\t\n715\t### T2.2 — Public-API integration test\n716\t\n717\t#### Objective\n718\tProve the composed public surface: import ONLY from `src/index.js` (the package entry),\n719\trender `<TheoTUIProvider><ChatMessage/></TheoTUIProvider>`, assert the frame.\n720\t\n721\t#### Why this step (action + reasoning)\n722\t\n723\t1. **What:** One integration test exercising provider + component + tokens through the\n724\t   composition root, exactly as a consumer would.\n725\t2. **Why now:** Unit tests (T1.1/T2.1) import modules directly; the integration boundary —\n726\t   \"the package entry re-exports a coherent working set\" — is untested until here. This is the\n727\t   wiring-triad pillar (b) for the milestone (integration test covering what unit mocks skipped —\n728\t   `rules/cycle-implement.md § Wiring triad`).\n729\t\n730\t#### Evidence\n731\t- Wiring-triad contract: `rules/cycle-implement.md § Wiring triad` (pillar b).\n732\t- Consumer-shaped import: manifest `exports` map defined in T0.1 (Blueprint\n733\t  §\"Coverage Corner 3 — Tools\").\n734\t\n735\t#### Files to edit\n736\t```\n737\ttests/public-api.integration.test.tsx — (NEW) provider+component via src/index.js\n738\t```\n739\t\n740\t#### Deep file dependency analysis\n741\t- Imports `{ TheoTUIProvider, ChatMessage, defaultTheme }` from `../src/index.js` + helper from\n742\t  `./helpers.tsx`. Depends on T1.1 + T2.1 exports; no production file changes.\n743\t\n744\t#### Deep Dives\n745\t- Asserts BOTH roles inside one provider with a custom theme override AND the defaultTheme path;\n746\t  mirrors the smallest real consumer program (same shape as `examples/basic.tsx` in T3.2).\n747\t- Edge case covered: nesting two ChatMessages under one provider (sibling render — Ink `Box`\n748\t  stacking) — asserts both lines present in frame.\n749\t\n750\t#### Tasks\n751\t1. Write the integration test (GREEN immediately is FORBIDDEN — first run it against a\n752\t   deliberately-broken import path to see it fail, then fix to the real path; records RED shape)\n753\t2. CHANGELOG entry (test-only — grouped under the T2 entry)\n754\t\n755\t#### TDD\n756\t```\n757\tRED:     public_entry_composes_provider_and_message_for_both_roles() — import from '../src/index.js'; render provider(custom user glyph)+2 messages; expect(frame).toContain(customGlyph); expect(frame).toContain(\"✦ \"); expect(frame).toContain both contents (RED via intentionally-wrong import first, then GREEN with real path)\n758\tGREEN:   Fix import to ../src/index.js — passes with no production change\n759\tREFACTOR: None expected\n760\tVERIFY:  pnpm vitest run tests/public-api.integration.test.tsx\n761\t```\n762\t\n763\t#### Concurrency tests\n764\t(none — single-threaded)\n765\t\n766\t#### Acceptance Criteria\n767\t- [ ] `pnpm vitest run tests/public-api.integration.test.tsx` exits 0 — imports resolve ONLY via `src/index.js`\n768\t- [ ] Pass: quality — `pnpm lint` exits 0; `wc -l tests/public-api.integration.test.tsx` <= 500\n769\t\n770\t#### DoD (Definition of Done)\n771\t- [ ] `pnpm gates` exits 0\n772\t\n773\t---\n774\t\n775\t## Phase 3: Benchmark + example (wiring triad closure)\n776\t\n777\t**Objective:** Render-benchmark harness with statistical protocol + committed baseline JSON +\n778\trunnable example — the milestone's runtime-observability pillar.\n779\t\n780\t### T3.1 — Benchmark harness + committed baseline\n781\t\n782\t#### Objective\n783\t`benchmarks/chat-message.bench.tsx` (workload) + `benchmarks/run.ts` (runner): renders a\n784\t100-message thread + 300-token streaming append into the last message via ink-testing-library;\n785\tcaptures frames + mean/peak/std-dev ms-per-frame over 1 warmup + ≥ 3 measured runs; writes\n786\t`docs/benchmarks/m0-chat-message-baseline.json` and prints a console table.\n787\t\n788\t#### Why this step (action + reasoning)\n789\t\n790\t1. **What:** Port react-ink's harness pattern to the M0 scale, upgraded with the statistical\n791\t   protocol (≥ 3 runs, mean ± std dev) and JSON persistence.\n792\t2. **Why now:** The cycle owner requires benchmark DATA as acceptance evidence; ADR D6/D9 lock\n793\t   the harness design + data-only posture. It lands after ChatMessage exists (it renders it) and\n794\t   before CI (which smoke-runs it).\n795\t\n796\t#### Evidence\n797\t- Harness prior art: `knowledge-base/references/assistant-ui/packages/react-ink/benchmarks/run.ts:19`\n798\t  (tsx spawn), `benchmarks/long-thread.bench.tsx:205-230` (frame-time sampling via\n799\t  `performance.now()` + `stdout.frames` deltas), `:272-341` (console reporting).\n800\t- Statistical protocol: `rules/analysis-golden-rule.md § 3` (≥ 3 runs, mean ± std dev, units +\n801\t  methodology + hardware).\n802\t- Artifact requirement: `rules/public-copy.md § 4` (reproducible benchmark artifact under\n803\t  `docs/benchmarks/`).\n804\t\n805\t#### Files to edit\n806\t```\n807\tbenchmarks/chat-message.bench.tsx — (NEW) workload + sampling + stats\n808\tbenchmarks/run.ts                 — (NEW) discovers *.bench.tsx, spawns via tsx, forwards exit code\n809\tdocs/benchmarks/m0-chat-message-baseline.json — (NEW) committed baseline (generated, then committed)\n810\tpackage.json                      — bench + bench:smoke scripts (declared in T0.1, verified here)\n811\t```\n812\t\n813\t#### Deep file dependency analysis\n814\t- `benchmarks/chat-message.bench.tsx`: imports `{ ChatMessage, TheoTUIProvider }` from\n815\t  `../src/index.js` (production caller — wiring pillar a) + `ink-testing-library` render.\n816\t- `benchmarks/run.ts`: fs/child_process only; forwards non-zero exit (fail-loud —\n817\t  `rules/error-handling.md § 2`).\n818\t- `docs/benchmarks/m0-chat-message-baseline.json`: consumed by future `/analysis` +\n819\t  M1 regression comparisons; `.prettierignore`d (T0.2) so format never rewrites data.\n820\t\n821\t#### Deep Dives\n822\t- Workload: build 100 `ChatMessage` elements (alternating roles) under one provider; then 300\n823\t  sequential `rerender()` calls appending a token to the last message's text (streaming\n824\t  simulation, no timers — synchronous re-render loop keeps the bench deterministic-ish and fast).\n825\t- Metrics per run: `frames` (stdout.frames.length delta), `mean_ms_per_frame`,\n826\t  `peak_ms_per_frame`; sampled via `performance.now()` around rerender batches — honest\n827\t  heuristic, same as prior art, noted in JSON `methodology` field.\n828\t- Protocol: 1 warmup (discarded) + N=5 measured runs (exceeds the ≥ 3 floor); aggregate\n829\t  `mean ± std_dev` per metric.\n830\t- JSON schema (exact fields): `{ benchmark, date, node_version, hardware: {cpu, cores},\n831\t  workload: {messages, streamed_tokens}, protocol: {warmup_runs, measured_runs},\n832\t  runs: [{frames, mean_ms_per_frame, peak_ms_per_frame}], aggregate: {frames_mean,\n833\t  mean_ms_per_frame: {mean, std_dev}, peak_ms_per_frame: {mean, std_dev}}, methodology }`.\n834\t- `--smoke` flag: 1 measured run, NO file write — CI proof-of-execution (ADR D9).\n835\t- Edge case: if `stdout.frames` yields 0 new frames for a batch (Ink coalescing), the sampler\n836\t  skips the empty delta (prior-art behavior, `long-thread.bench.tsx:210` `if (newFrames <= 0) return`).\n837\t\n838\t#### Pseudo-code / Signatures\n839\t```pseudocode\n840\tasync function runOnce(): RunMetrics\n841\t  instance = render(<App messages=100 />)         -- inside ink-testing-library\n842\t  t0 = performance.now(); framesSeen = instance.stdout.frames.length\n843\t  for token in 1..300:\n844\t    rerender(<App messages=100 streamedTokens=token />)\n845\t    sample(instance, now())                        -- frame-delta sampling\n846\t  return { frames, mean_ms_per_frame, peak_ms_per_frame }\n847\t\n848\tmain: warmup(); runs = repeat(5, runOnce); writeJSON(aggregate(runs)) unless --smoke\n849\t\n850\t# Example aggregate output (real numbers land at implement)\n851\t{ \"mean_ms_per_frame\": { \"mean\": 3.1, \"std_dev\": 0.4 }, ... }\n852\t```\n853\t\n854\t#### Tasks\n855\t1. RED test: `benchmarks/run.test.ts`-shaped assertion is NOT used — instead the RED gate is the"}]},"uuid":"b4843a98-d62a-4921-8ae1-06edfc44fe36","timestamp":"2026-07-06T11:22:22.567Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/paulo/Projetos/usetheo/theokit-tools/theokit-tui/.claude/knowledge-base/plans/m0-walking-skeleton-plan.md","content":"---\nslug: m0-walking-skeleton\nmilestone_id: M0\ncreated_at: 2026-07-05\ngoal: Ship the @theokit/tui walking skeleton (ChatMessage + TheoTUIProvider, ESM-only) with all five gates green in CI and a committed render-benchmark baseline.\n---\n\n# Plan: M0 Walking Skeleton — @theokit/tui scaffold + ChatMessage primitive\n\n> **Version 1.2** (v1.1 absorbed edge cases; v1.2 self-contains ADRs D1–D6, executable criteria oracles, deps-audit resolutions; /plan-confidence SHIPPABLE 91.6) (absorbs MUST FIX EC-1/EC-2 + SHOULD TEST EC-3/EC-4/EC-5 from\n> `reviews/m0-walking-skeleton-plan-edge-cases-2026-07-05.md`) — Bootstraps `@theokit/tui` from an empty (pre-code) repository into a\n> publishable-shape Ink 5 component library: strict-TS ESM-only package built with tsup, tested\n> with vitest + ink-testing-library (deterministic snapshots), one primitive (`ChatMessage`,\n> user/assistant) wrapped by a `<TheoTUIProvider>` token stub, Apache-2.0 licensing, a CI gate\n> chain (format → lint → typecheck → test → build), and a render-benchmark harness whose baseline\n> (mean ± std dev over ≥ 3 runs) is committed as JSON. Implements `ROADMAP.md § M0` under the\n> decisions locked in the m0-walking-skeleton blueprint (SHIPPABLE 99.5).\n\n## Goal\n\nEnable TypeScript agent-CLI developers to render a `ChatMessage` (user/assistant) inside\n`<TheoTUIProvider>` from the built `@theokit/tui` package so that the M0 walking skeleton is\nproven end-to-end, measured by the CI gate chain `format → lint → typecheck → test → build`\nexiting 0 on `develop`.\n\n## Context\n\nThe repository is pre-code: only `ROADMAP.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.gitignore` and the `.claude/` kit\nexist (see Baseline Context). `ROADMAP.md § M0` defines the walking skeleton as the thinnest\nvertical slice proving the whole toolchain; every scaffold decision here is load-bearing for\nM1–M8. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (NEW) | 0 | — | (CI gate chain) | — |\n\nReference (not modified): `ROADMAP.md` (217 LoC, `b77ff73`), `CLAUDE.md` (69 LoC, `fee8df7`).\n\n### Current callers / dependents\n\n(none — pre-code repository; no existing production symbol is modified. The new public symbols\n`ChatMessage`, `TheoTUIProvider`, `useTheoTheme`, `defaultTheme` gain their first callers inside\nthis plan: `examples/basic.tsx`, `benchmarks/chat-message.bench.tsx`, and the test files listed\nabove.)\n\n### Domain glossary\n\n- **primitive** — one exported terminal component of the AI-agent surface (here: `ChatMessage`).\n- **walking skeleton** — thinnest end-to-end slice proving the whole toolchain, not a feature.\n- **frame** — one Ink render flush to stdout; `ink-testing-library` exposes `lastFrame()`/`frames`.\n- **semantic token** — named theme value (`role.user.prefix`) decoupled from raw color.\n- **TTFATT** — time-to-first-agent-turn-in-terminal; north-star metric (`ROADMAP.md § Success criteria`).\n- **gate chain** — `format → lint → typecheck → test → build`, each exiting 0.\n\n### Architecture boundaries affected\n\nPer `rules/architecture.md § 1` (composition root at the top) and `§ 3` (module cohesion):\n`src/index.ts` is the package's composition root — the only public surface (`exports` map exposes\na single `.` entry). `src/theme.tsx` is the domain layer (tokens = contract; provider = wiring at\nthe boundary); `src/chat-message.tsx` is interface-layer (rendering), consuming the theme ONLY via\n`useTheoTheme()` (DIP: component depends on the token contract, not on literal colors). No adapter\nor external-I/O layer exists at M0.\n\n## Prior Art & Related Work\n\n- **Internal blueprint:** `knowledge-base/discoveries/blueprints/m0-walking-skeleton-blueprint.md` —\n  ADRs D1–D6 are consumed verbatim by this plan (§ ADRs below maps them);
